True = prime candidate\\n    small_sieve[0] = small_sieve[1] = 0\\n\\n    for i in range(2, limit):\\n        if small_sieve[i]:\\n            base_primes.append(i)\\n            small_sieve[i*i::i] = bytearray(len(small_sieve[i*i::i]))\\n\\n    yield from base_primes\\n\\n    # Step 2: Process segments beyond √n\\n    # Segment size chosen to fit L1/L2 cache (~32KB-64KB); For $N=10^7$: $\\\\sqrt{N} \\\\approx 3162$ base primes + 32 KB segment ≈ **~35 KB total**, versus ~10 MB for a full boolean array or ~280 MB for a Python `list[bool]` |\\n\\n### Key Design Decisions for Memory Efficiency\\n\\n1.  **`bytearray` over `list[bool]`**: Python `bool` objects are full heap-allocated objects (~28 bytes each). `bytearray` stores raw C `unsigned char`, giving an **~28× memory reduction** for sieve arrays.\\n2.  **Fixed segment size aligned to cache**: The 32 KB segment fits within typical L1 data caches, avoiding cache misses that dominate runtime for $N > 10^7$.
